# guilda 0.2.0 — process

Status: spec approved by the user on 2026-11-10 (R3, gate G0). The scope is `@decided` (release plan of 2026-11-09,
and the checklist of section 5 that the user confirmed on 2026-11-10). One item of section 5 is open: the curation
of the lab skills (D36). It is gate G2 of the plan.
Decisions: [decision register](2026-11-09-guilda-decisions.md). Earlier release: [0.1.1](2026-11-09-guilda-0.1.1.md).
Later releases: [backlog](2026-11-09-guilda-backlog.md).
Base: the released 0.1.1 (tag `guilda--v0.1.1`). In this spec, "today" is that code. A line says so when it is
about the development branch of 0.2.0.

## 1. Intent

Field use of 0.1.0 in three projects showed four problems:

- The law rules did not run in two sessions, and nothing told the user (#2, #11).
- The process is too heavy for small and personal projects (#2, #13).
- Specs and briefs had gaps that the Executor or the Reviewer found late. Each gap cost about one task cycle (#2, #4,
  #5, #8, #9).
- A field report from a project reaches this repository only when someone writes it by hand (#12).

0.2.0 fixes these problems. It is a minor release, because core rules change meaning (0.x rule of `RELEASING.md`).

### Labs that gate parts of 0.2.0

Labs are part of the development process (release plan of the register, 2026-11-10). The plan of 0.2.0 runs each lab
below as a task in a dispatched agent (R26). The lab task comes before the task that builds the part that it gates. A lab with NO-GO or
MORE DATA stops that part, and the part goes to the user. One part has a decided fallback: the approval part of the
production lock (O4, below).

| Lab | Gates | PASS criteria |
|---|---|---|
| AI artifact gate (D29) | the gate of D29 in the Reviewer and QA | a sample prompt with a tool; the suite at 0.8 or more in 3 runs; planted bad versions rejected |
| Architect split (D30) | the core Architect file and its reference files | 5 fixed situations, one file against the split, 3 runs each; at least as correct, 25% fewer tokens or more, the correct reference in 14 of 15 cases |
| Skill hook (D43) | the hook that refuses a skill outside the group of the agent, with its rule R30 (S35), and the `Skill` part of R28 (S41). This lab runs before the R28 task | a Claude Code hook sees one specific skill call and refuses it. The hook input names the agent type of the calling agent. It shows no agent type for the main session. The lab records the exact `skill` value of the hook input for a core skill and for an extension skill. It records whether the installed core folder holds `plugins/guilda-skills/` (section 3e). The Architect writes the other criteria before the run (D19) |
| Extension detection (O1, D2, D40) | the extension check of the core (S13) and the extension line of `doctor` (S21) | a core hook or `doctor` tells, at session start, whether the extension plugin loaded, in a session with the extension loaded and in a session with only the core loaded (`claude --plugin-dir`; the case of a config with no marketplace is the manual check of S13); candidates: a marker file that the extension writes, `claude plugin list`; the Architect writes the criteria before the run (D19) |
| Production commands (O7, D42) | the production lock and its rule R29 (S34) | a DevOps lab (DevOps is in the Engineering group, D42), with research and an experiment in a dispatched agent, decides how the hook recognizes a production command (deploy, rollback, an infrastructure change); the Architect writes the criteria before the run (D19) |
| Production approval (O4, D42), in the same DevOps lab as O7 | the approval part of the production lock (S34) | the lab shows how the hook sees the user's approval of one production command; the Architect writes the criteria before the run (D19). On a NO-GO or MORE DATA, the fallback below applies |

The fallback for O4 (the user, 2026-11-10): no agent ever runs a production command. The hook refuses each
production command that it recognizes, from each agent. The agent prepares the command. The user runs it, for
example with `!` in the prompt. Not verified: that a command that the user runs with `!` does not pass the
PreToolUse hook. The lab checks it.

## 3. Rules that change meaning

The CHANGELOG entry of 0.2.0 lists these rules under "Changed". The rules change in place (D40): the project layer
copies no rule text, so no project runs `guilda:init` again. The user approved each text below on 2026-11-10
(section 5).

Each block is in the form of `core/rules.md`: one heading, then one `Rule:`, one `Why:` and one `Check:` line. A
`Check:` line is one line, also when it is long. The linter result of each block is under the block. The command:
copy the block to a temporary file, then run `node scripts/lint-rules.mjs --prefix <R, ARC or EXE> <file>`.

### R24 — Review cycle by process level (D1)

```
### R24 — One review cycle for each review unit
Rule:  Give each plan task its own Executor ↔ Reviewer cycle at `full`, and one review of the integration at `light`.
Why:   A cycle that covers several tasks hides defects in a large diff, and a `light` project accepts that risk by choice.
Check: gate — At `full`, the Reviewer rejects a diff that holds more than one plan task, and each task closes only on a written approval of the Reviewer. At `light`, the controller closes the integration only on a written approval of the Reviewer.
```

Linter: pass (exit 0). The earlier text had two sentences in `Rule:` and failed.

(2026-11-10, the user) The `Check:` line has again the sentence of 0.1.0: at `full`, each task closes only on a
written approval of the Reviewer. Pass 2 of `agents/reviewer.md` cites R24 for it.

### R11 — Rejections counted for each review unit (D1, D8)

```
### R11 — Small or large rejection; 3 rejections escalate
Rule:  Send small rejections to the Executor, large to the Architect, and the third to the Architect and the user.
Why:   A large fix changes a spec scenario, an Acceptance criterion, a contract or the file list, and only the Architect owns them.
Check: gate — The verdict states `REJECTED-SMALL` or `REJECTED-LARGE`. The controller counts the rejections of each review unit: a task at `full`, the integration at `light`. A verdict with `Stage: spec` does not count. QA sends its report to the Reviewer, never to the Executor.
```

Linter: pass (exit 0). The `Check:` line is now one line.

### R22 — no change

D1 cited R22 for "QA for each task". R22 is QA in two phases. QA at `light` still runs both phases, one time at the
end. The text of R22 stays.

### R13 — Questions through the question tool (D23)

```
### R13 — Decisions through the question tool
Rule:  Ask each decision in the question tool in plain words, with context, 2 to 4 options and the recommended first.
Why:   The context (where, what, a concrete scenario, the effect of each option) lets the user decide; a decision in a long reply was missed five times, and a rule ID with no meaning was not understood.
Check: judgment — No hook and no Reviewer sees the questions, so this rule is text only.
```

Linter: pass (exit 0). Approved 2026-11-10 with the plain-words addition. The earlier text had a `Rule:` of two lines and failed. In Claude Code, the question tool is
`AskUserQuestion`.

### ARC1 — One decision at a time in the question tool (D23)

```
### ARC1 — One decision at a time
Rule:  Ask each decision of the user alone in the question tool, never only in the reply text, and follow R13.
Why:   A list of questions gets partial answers, and a decision in the reply text was not seen five times.
Check: judgment — No hook and no Reviewer sees the questions, so this rule is text only.
```

Linter: pass (exit 0). The earlier `Rule:` had 23 words and failed.

### EXE2 — The new test fails against the old code (D10)

```
### EXE2 — Tests fail first, against the old code
Rule:  Run each new test against the old code, and record its failure output in the final answer.
Why:   A test that never failed on the old code can pass for the wrong reason and proves nothing.
Check: gate — The Reviewer rejects a final answer with no failure output of a new test against the old code.
```

Linter: pass (exit 0). No change to the earlier proposal.

### R4 — A plan section or a plan file (D27)

```
### R4 — One MD per feature; the plan is a section or one plan file
Rule:  Write each plan as a `## Plan: Part N` section of its feature MD, or in `<feature>.plan.md` next to it.
Why:   One place for each plan keeps the spec, its Acceptance scenarios and its plan in step.
Check: law — PreToolUse refuses a new file in `docs/superpowers/plans/`, and a new `*.plan.md` file whose `<feature>.md` is not in the same folder.
```

Linter: pass (exit 0). The `Check:` line is now one line.

The hook reads three tools (the user, 2026-11-10). In 0.1.1 the hook checks only `Write` and `Edit`. In the live
smoke test of 0.1.1, a plan file made through `Bash` passed. An example is `cat > docs/superpowers/plans/x.md`.
0.2.0 closes this gap in the same change of the R4 hook:

- The `Bash` path of `scripts/hooks/pre-tool-use.mjs` reads the command text. It finds each file that the command
  creates in a common shell form. The forms (the Architect's choice): a redirection (`>`, `>>`), `tee`, `cp`, `mv`
  and `touch`.
- The hook refuses the command when such a file is a new file in `docs/superpowers/plans/`, or a new orphan
  `*.plan.md`. The refusal cites R4 and records a violation, as for `Write`.
- A relative path (the Architect's choice, fifth cold review): the hook resolves it from two directories. The
  first is the project folder, as the `Write` check does today (`isNewPlanFile`). The second is the directory of
  the command. That directory starts at the `cwd` of the hook input. Each `cd` or `pushd` with a literal target
  moves it, as `findGitCommits` of `scripts/lib/git-command.mjs` does for R19. The hook refuses the command when
  one of the two results is a plan file.
- The limit of `cd`: the hook does not follow a `cd` whose target has a variable. It does not follow `popd`.
  Then it uses the directory before that `cd`, and the project folder.
- This check runs before the R19 commit scan. Today the `Bash` path returns early when the command names no
  `git commit`.
- The limit: the hook reads only the command text. It catches the common forms, not each form. It does not see a
  path that comes from a variable or from a command substitution. It does not see a write from inside a script or an
  interpreter, such as `node -e` or `python -c`. So a plan file can still pass the hook.
- The `Check:` line does not change, because it names no tool.

## 3a. New rules

These rules are new, so no rule changes meaning. The user approved R26, R27 and ARC7 to ARC10 on 2026-11-10
(section 5). R28 is new from the user's decision of 2026-11-10 (register, D40). The user approved its text on
2026-11-10 (section 5). R29 and R30 give a rule to the two new laws of D42 and D43. The user approved both texts on 2026-11-10 (section 5).

### R26 — Labs run in a dispatched agent (D19)

R26 is the next free core ID after R25. The core rule of D19 gets the ID R26.

```
### R26 — Labs run in a dispatched agent
Rule:  Run each lab, experiment or spike in a dispatched agent, never in the controller session.
Why:   The controller writes the hypothesis and gives the verdict, so a lab that it runs itself grades its own work and fills its context.
Check: gate — The Reviewer rejects a lab report that has no agent ID of the agent that ran the lab (D34).
```

Linter: pass (exit 0).

### R27 — Truth first; only the leads disagree (D46)

```
### R27 — Truth first; only the leads disagree
Rule:  Report the evidence with its limits, never make a result look better, and send each objection to your lead.
Why:   A grader that preferred a fake success to an honest failure built a wrong product (D39), and only a lead argues with the user (D46).
Check: judgment — No check can see truth yet, so a Lab study (D39, D46) refines this rule in a later release.
```

Linter: pass (exit 0). The leads are the Architect and the lead of each group. Until 0.5.0 the
`security` agent is the lead of Security. R14 (the Architect disagrees) stays.

### R28 — Tell the user when guilda is not set up (D40)

R28 is the next free core ID after R27. The user decided the law and the notice text on 2026-11-10. The user decided
again after the third cold review (2026-11-10). The rule text states only what is certain. That is the session
start and the dispatch of a `guilda:*` agent. The `Skill` part enters the text only on a GO of the D43 lab.

```
### R28 — Tell the user when guilda is not set up
Rule:  With no `.guilda/` folder, tell the user that guilda is not set up, and run no other law.
Why:   A plugin that loads and does nothing looks like it works, so the user trusts checks that never ran (#2, #11).
Check: law — SessionStart, and PreToolUse on the dispatch of a `guilda:*` agent, print the set-up notice.
```

Linter: pass (exit 0). The notice text: "guilda is installed but not set up in this project. If you just installed it,
reload; then run guilda:init. Without it guilda does not work."

- Channel and frequency (the Architect's choice): the session start prints the notice once per session. It prints
  it when the source is `startup` or `clear`. It goes in `additionalContext` for the model and in `systemMessage`
  for the user. The PreToolUse hook prints it in `systemMessage` at each `Agent` call of a `guilda:*` agent. It
  never refuses a call.
- The `Skill` part depends on the same unknown as the D43 lab: whether a hook sees one skill call. So the D43 lab
  runs before the R28 task, and it gates the `Skill` part. On a GO, the `Check:` line becomes the line below. Then
  `scripts/hooks/pre-skill.mjs` prints the notice at each `Skill` call of a `guilda:` skill. The one exception is
  `guilda:init`. On a NO-GO or MORE DATA, the text above stays, and `scripts/hooks/pre-skill.mjs` is not built.

  ```
  Check: law — SessionStart, and PreToolUse on a guilda skill or a `guilda:*` agent, print the set-up notice.
  ```

  Linter: pass (exit 0) for the block with this line. It is the line that the user approved first on 2026-11-10.
- Not verified: that `systemMessage` of a PreToolUse hook that does not refuse shows to the user. The build checks it
  on the current Claude Code before the task closes.
- Release: 0.2.0, because `RELEASING.md` says that a new rule raises the minor number (0.1.1 spec, Q1).

### R29 — Production commands need the user's approval (D42)

`@decided` (2026-11-10, the user; the Architect proposed it in the fifth cold review). The production lock of D42
is a law, and a law needs a rule. R29 is the next free core ID after R28.

```
### R29 — Production commands need the user's approval
Rule:  Never run a production command (a deploy, a rollback, an infrastructure change) without the explicit approval of the user.
Why:   A production command changes a live system for its users, and only the user owns that risk (R12).
Check: law — PreToolUse refuses a production command that it recognizes, unless the DevOps agent runs it with the approval of the user.
```

Linter: pass (exit 0).

- The O4 lab picks the `Check:` line. On a GO, the line above stays. On a NO-GO or MORE DATA, the fallback of
  section 2 applies, and the `Check:` line becomes the line below. The `Rule:` line is true in both cases.

  ```
  Check: law — PreToolUse refuses each production command that it recognizes, from each agent.
  ```

  Linter: pass (exit 0) for the block with this line.
- The block enters `core/rules.md` in the task that builds the lock, after the DevOps lab. On a NO-GO or MORE DATA
  of the O7 part, the lock stops and goes to the user (section 2). Then the block does not enter the file.
- The refusal text starts with `R29:`. It tells the agent to give the command to the user.
- The violation record has the `rule` `R29`, the `level` `law` and the `action` `production command refused`.
  Its `source` is the file name of the hook without `.mjs`. The record never has the command text, because a
  command can have a secret.
- The project layer states are in section 3d.

### R30 — Skills by role group (D43)

`@decided` (2026-11-10, the user; the Architect proposed it in the fifth cold review). The skill-group hook of D43
is a law, and a law needs a rule. R30 is the next free core ID after R29.

```
### R30 — Skills by role group
Rule:  Load a guilda skill only when its `groups` list has your role group or the value `shared`.
Why:   A skill of another group gives an agent methods that its role must not use, such as `red-team` in an Engineering Executor.
Check: law — PreToolUse refuses a `Skill` call of a guilda skill from an agent that has no role group, or no group of the skill.
```

Linter: pass (exit 0).

- The block enters `core/rules.md` only on a GO of the D43 lab, in the task that builds the hook. On a NO-GO or
  MORE DATA, the hook stops and goes to the user (section 2). Then the block does not enter the file.
- The hook is `scripts/hooks/pre-skill.mjs`, the same file as the `Skill` part of R28. Each refusal text starts
  with `R30:`, not with `D43:` (section 3e).
- The violation record has the `rule` `R30`, the `level` `law` and the `source` `pre-skill`. Its `action` is
  `skill call refused: <skill name>`.
- The project layer states are in section 3d.

The session text for R29 and R30: the session start prints `core/rules.md`, so the main session reads both rules.
A subagent reads `core/subagent.md`. That file has no law block today, also not for R4 and R19. So a subagent
learns the rule from the refusal text, which has the rule ID and the next step.

### ARC7, ARC8, ARC10 — Concrete specs (D6, issue #5)

```
### ARC7 — Concrete scenarios
Rule:  Write each Acceptance scenario with its input data, exact expected result, QA tool and test name.
Why:   A vague scenario gives a green test that proves nothing, and QA cannot fail it.
Check: gate — The Reviewer rejects a scenario without an exact result or with a word from the vague list.

### ARC8 — One owner task for each decision
Rule:  Give each `@decided` item exactly one owner task in the traceability table before the briefs ship.
Why:   A decision without an owner is not built, and two owners build it two times in two ways.
Check: gate — The Reviewer rejects a plan whose table misses a `@decided` item or gives it two owners.

### ARC10 — Glossary for each new term
Rule:  Add each new domain term of a spec to `docs/superpowers/specs/GLOSSARY.md` with its forbidden synonyms.
Why:   Two names for one thing give two meanings in the code, the tests and the docs (R16).
Check: gate — The Reviewer rejects a spec that uses a domain term that the glossary does not define.
```

Linter: pass (exit 0) for each block. The texts are the texts of issue #5.

### ARC9 — A mutant for each new test (D7, issue #5)

```
### ARC9 — A mutant for each new test
Rule:  At `full`, name for each new test one change to the product code that makes the test fail.
Why:   A test that no product change can break guards nothing, and it stays green while the product breaks.
Check: gate — At `full`, and at `light` when the brief has a `Mutants:` field, the Reviewer applies each mutant in a temporary copy and classifies it as killed, survived or equivalent with a reason. A survived mutant is a rejection, an equivalent mutant is only noted, and a kill counts only when the failing assertion targets the mutant.
```

Linter: pass (exit 0). The text of issue #5 has no level and no classification. This text adds both from D7: required
at `full`, the Architect's choice at `light`, and the classification of #10.

How the Architect marks a security zone (D1, the Architect's choice): the spec block has the line
`Security zone: <risk area>` directly under its heading. The value is a risk area of D1 or of
`config.security.riskAreas`. A task that builds a block with this line gets a brief whose `Risk area:` is not
`none`. That field sends the task to Security today. A block with no such line is not a zone.

What `explain` changes (D23; the user, 2026-11-10): the Architect reads the value in the `Explain:` part of the
session context line. At `full`, each question has the where, the what, a concrete example and the effect of each
option. At `brief`, each question has one sentence of context and the options. `guilda:init` asks which one. R13
holds at both values. No hook and no script reads the value to change its own behavior.

Found with `grep -rnE '\b(R24|R11|R13|EXE2|ARC1|R4)\b'` outside `docs/`. The design history in `docs/` does not change.

### Glossary proposals for 0.2.0

Approved by the user on 2026-11-10 (section 5). Each line has the format of `core/glossary.md`.

| Term | Line |
|---|---|
| process level | `- **process level**: the value of the process field in .guilda/config.json, light or full, that sets the review cycle and the gates but never a law. Forbidden: process mode, process profile.` |
| security zone | `- **security zone**: a spec block that the Architect marks because it touches a risk area, and that gets a Security review at each process level. Forbidden: hot spot, sensitive area.` |
| review unit | `- **review unit**: the work that one Reviewer cycle covers and that the rejections count against: a task at full, the integration at light. Forbidden: review batch, review scope.` |
| domain skill | `- **domain skill**: a skill with the knowledge of one area that a brief names in its Skills field, and that the Executor and the Reviewer both load. Forbidden: specialist agent, area agent.` |
| triage | `- **triage**: the step before each brief that sends an open question to the Lab, a risk area to Security, and a decided change to Engineering. Forbidden: task routing, intake.` |
| mutant | `- **mutant**: one named change to the product code that makes a new test fail, classified as killed, survived or equivalent. Forbidden: bug injection, fault seed.` |

The register has the lines for `field report`, `lab report` and `Lab group`. The `supervisor` line is approved, and
it enters `core/glossary.md` in 0.5.0.

The forbidden words are phrases that guilda text does not use for another thing. `ste-check` matches a forbidden
word in any case and in any text, so a common word such as "mode" or "tier" cannot be forbidden.

## 3c. The core plugin and the extension plugin (D40)

The user decided the split on 2026-11-10. The marketplace lists three plugins: the core, the extension and the
optional tool plugin `ios-on-linux` (D26). The agents stay in the core plugin `guilda`. So their names
(`guilda:executor`, `guilda:reviewer`, `guilda:qa`, `guilda:security`, `guilda:version-control`) and the hook
matchers of `hooks/hooks.json` do not change. The extension holds only what needs `skill-creator` and `superpowers`.

| Part | Core plugin `guilda` | Extension plugin `guilda-skills` |
|---|---|---|
| Manifest | `.claude-plugin/plugin.json`, no `dependencies` | `plugins/guilda-skills/.claude-plugin/plugin.json`, with `skill-creator` and `superpowers` and version ranges (the entry form is below) |
| Laws and hooks | `hooks/hooks.json`, `scripts/hooks/`, `scripts/lib/` | no law. One exception: when the lab of O1 picks the marker file, the extension has one SessionStart hook, and that hook only writes the marker |
| Rules | `core/` | none |
| Agents | `agents/`: the five 0.1.0 agents, the DevOps advisor (D42), the AI lead (D45), the Architect executor, the coherence validator and the three lab roles (section 3e) | none |
| User skills | `skills/architect`, `skills/init`, `skills/update`, `skills/report` | none |
| Other skills | `skills/lab` (`guilda:lab`, not a user command) | the domain skills of D25, D28, D29, D36, D42 and D45, one folder each under `plugins/guilda-skills/skills/` |
| Agent creator (D41) | none | the agent creator skill (D41), because it builds on `skill-creator` |
| Scripts | `scripts/doctor.mjs`, `scripts/report-send.mjs`, `scripts/bump-version.mjs` (the release step, section 3g), and the 0.1.0 scripts | none |

One repository and one marketplace (the Architect's choice):

```
.claude-plugin/marketplace.json   lists "guilda" (source "./"), the extension (source "./plugins/guilda-skills")
                                  and "ios-on-linux" (source "./plugins/ios-on-linux")
.claude-plugin/plugin.json        the core manifest
agents/  core/  hooks/  scripts/  skills/   the core
plugins/guilda-skills/
  .claude-plugin/plugin.json      the extension manifest
  skills/<domain skill>/SKILL.md
plugins/ios-on-linux/
  .claude-plugin/plugin.json      the tool plugin manifest, no dependency
  skills/ios-on-linux/SKILL.md
tests/  docs/  evals/             stay at the root
```

- The core stays at the repository root, so the marketplace source of a 0.1.x install (`./`) does not change.
- The core folder holds `plugins/guilda-skills/` as a subfolder. Claude Code loads plugin components from the root
  folders of the plugin only. So the core does not load the extension skills. Not verified: the build checks it with
  `claude plugin validate --strict .` and a session with the core alone loaded with `--plugin-dir` (L6, P2).
- `ios-on-linux` (D26, the Architect's choice): the skill is not in this repository today. Its task copies the
  user's existing `ios-on-linux` skill into `plugins/ios-on-linux/skills/ios-on-linux/`. The copy has no machine
  path and no personal data (R19). The plugin has no dependency, and no other plugin requires it.

Dependency entries of the extension manifest (the Architect's choice):

- No released version has a dependency. The development branch of 0.2.0 has one entry in the core manifest. It
  is a string with no version: `"skill-creator@claude-plugins-official"`.
- The proposed form with a range: `{ "name": "superpowers", "marketplace": "claude-plugins-official", "version": "^7.0.0" }`,
  and the same form for `skill-creator`. Not verified: that the manifest accepts this form. A check in the build
  runs `claude plugin validate --strict` and loads a throwaway plugin with it (`--plugin-dir`; L6, P1). No agent
  installs a plugin. `skill-creator` has no `version` field in its manifest today, so its entry uses the string
  form (below) from the start.
- The fallback: the string form with no range. Then `guilda:init` reads the installed version of each dependency
  and tells the user when it is outside the range.
- `.claude-plugin/marketplace.json` gets `allowCrossMarketplaceDependenciesOn` for the extension. The released
  0.1.1 does not have the field. It lists the marketplace of each dependency. The first entry is
  `claude-plugins-official`. That marketplace serves `superpowers` 7.0.0 (seen in an install on 2026-11-10). If the
  README install uses the own marketplace of `superpowers`, its name enters the list too. That name is not
  verified.

## 3d. Project layer states, safe defaults and migrations (D40)

### States

| State | How the hook sees it | Laws R4, R18, R19, ARC6 | Session start |
|---|---|---|---|
| no project layer | no `.guilda/` folder | do not run (the user, 2026-11-10) | the R28 notice only; no core rules load |
| broken project layer | a `.guilda/` folder, and `config.json` is missing, unreadable, not JSON, or fails the schema check | run with the safe defaults (fail closed; the fix ships in 0.1.1) | the core rules and `.guilda/rules.md` load, with the safe defaults and one notice |
| valid project layer | the config passes the schema check after the migrations | run with the config | as in 0.1.0, with the migrated config |

The two new laws, R29 (the production lock) and R30 (the skill groups), follow the same table (`@decided` 2026-11-10,
section 3a):

| State | R29 and R30 hooks |
|---|---|
| no project layer | do not run. guilda is not set up, so by R28 no other law runs. The R28 notice shows |
| broken project layer | run (fail closed). The two hooks read no value of `config.json`, so they work as in a valid layer |
| valid project layer | run |

### Required and defaulted fields (the Architect's choice)

| Kind | Fields | Missing | Invalid value |
|---|---|---|---|
| required | `guildaVersion`, `languages` (`replies`, `docs`), `tiers` (`master`, `journeyman`, `apprentice`), `writing`, `security`, `checks` (the 0.1.0 `checkSchema`) | broken project layer | broken project layer |
| defaulted | `schemaVersion` (1), `process` (`full`), `explain` (`full`), `scan` (`ignore: []`), `report` (`repo`: the `repository` value of the core manifest) | the default; the layer stays valid | `scan`: broken project layer. `process`, `explain`: fail closed to `full`, the layer stays valid, and one notice. `report.repo`: `scripts/report-send.mjs` saves the report and sends nothing |

The notice for an invalid `process` or `explain`: "guilda: .guilda/config.json has an invalid <field> value; guilda
uses full. Run guilda:update."

The hooks read with tolerance, and the two scripts that the user's flow runs are strict. Two functions keep the two
behaviors apart:

- `checkSchema` stays as it is today. It checks the required fields and `scan`. `loadConfig` calls it in each
  hook. It does not check `process`, `explain` or `report`. So an invalid value of these never gives a broken
  project layer.
- `checkStrict` is new, in `scripts/lib/project.mjs`. It runs `checkSchema`, then it checks the defaulted fields.
  `scripts/config-write.mjs` and `scripts/config-check.mjs` call it. They refuse an invalid `process`, `explain` or
  `report.repo` value and exit 1.
- `projectState` normalizes the value. It changes an invalid `process` or `explain` to `full`. It returns the
  notice text in a `notices` list. Only the session start prints the notices.

A refusal in a project with no `.guilda/` folder records nothing and creates no folder (0.1.1, S14).

### The context line of the level (D1, the Architect's choice)

- Session: `scripts/hooks/session-start.mjs` prints one new line after the `Languages:` line. Its form is
  `Process: <light or full>. Explain: <brief or full>.`
- Subagent: `scripts/hooks/subagent-start.mjs` prints `Docs language: <docs>. Process: <light or full>. Glossary: <path>`.
  Today the line has no `Process:` part.
- The values come from `projectState`. So a 0.1.x config and a broken project layer both give `Process: full.`

### Safe defaults (the Architect's choice)

| Field | Safe value | Reason |
|---|---|---|
| `tiers` | the values of `core/tiers.json` | the default of ARC4 today |
| `languages.replies`, `languages.docs` | `en` | the language of the plugin text |
| `process` | `full` | the stricter level, and the behavior of 0.1.0 |
| `explain` | `full` | a question with its full context is the safer side of D23 |
| `security` | `enabled: true`, `riskAreas: []` | a Security review of a risk area stays on |
| `scan.ignore` | `[]` | the R19 scan reads each staged file |
| `writing`, `checks` | `free: []`, `exceptions: []`; `checks: []` | no exception, and no check command is guessed |

The core rules load in a broken project layer, because the laws run there and the model must know them. The notice
text: "guilda: <reason>. guilda runs with safe defaults. Run guilda:init to fix .guilda/config.json." The notice
shows once per session, as R28 does.

### Migration contract

- `schemaVersion` lives in `.guilda/config.json`. A 0.1.x config has no `schemaVersion`, and the reader reads it
  as 1. A 0.2.0 config has `schemaVersion: 2`.
- Who writes `schemaVersion: 2` (the Architect's choice): `scripts/config-write.mjs`. It adds the field to each new
  config, as it adds `guildaVersion`. The template `skills/init/references/config-template.json` does not have the
  field. The number is one constant in `scripts/lib/project.mjs`.
- An existing config: `node scripts/config-write.mjs <projectDir> --migrate` reads the config and runs the
  migrations. It writes the result with `schemaVersion: 2` and the installed `guildaVersion`. It changes no other
  value. `guilda:update` runs it after the user approves (S43).
- `--migrate` refuses three cases (the Architect's choice, fifth cold review). In each one it prints one line on
  standard error, exits 1 and changes no byte of the file:
  - a broken project layer: `migrate refused: <reason>. Run guilda:init to fix .guilda/config.json.`
  - an invalid `process` or `explain` value: `migrate refused: invalid <field> value`. The script does not write
    `full` over a value that the user typed.
  - a `schemaVersion` higher than 2: `migrate refused: schemaVersion <n> is newer than this guilda`. The script
    never downgrades a config.
- The migrations from 1 to 2: a missing `process` becomes `full`; a missing `explain` becomes `full`; the
  `schemaVersion` becomes 2. Each migration is safe to run again.
- Each hook is its own process. So `projectState` (on top of `loadConfig`) runs the migrations in memory at each
  call, in each hook. Only the session start prints a notice.
- A migration writes the committed `.guilda/config.json` only through `guilda:update`, or after the user approves the
  write in a conversation.
- A hook never asks a question. A value that no migration can infer becomes one session start notice. The notice
  sends the user to `guilda:update`. Until then, the hook uses the safe default. Each value of 1 to 2 can be
  inferred. So 0.2.0 has no case of this path, and no test.
- The reader may not know the `schemaVersion` of a config. Then the tolerant reader reads it and ignores the
  unknown fields. The layer stays valid. The notice: "guilda: .guilda/config.json has schemaVersion <n>, newer
  than this guilda knows. Run guilda:update."

### The version notice (D40, the Architect's choice)

Today `versionNotice` in `scripts/hooks/session-start.mjs` has two texts. Both tell the user to run `guilda:init`.
0.2.0 has one text, for a patch, a minor or a major update:

"guilda updated <configured> → <installed>. The project layer works as it is. Run guilda:update to see the changes
and to save the new version."

- The notice shows while `guildaVersion` in the config is lower than the installed version.
- `skills/update/SKILL.md` changes with it. Its steps 6 and 9 stop telling the user to run `guilda:init` again. A
  new last step offers the write of the migrated config with `--migrate`. Hard limit 3 allows this one write to
  `.guilda/config.json`, after the user says yes. When the plugin is up to date and the config is older, the skill
  goes straight to that step.
- The description of `skills/update/SKILL.md` adds the trigger "a session notice says Run guilda:update".

## 3e. Role groups in files (D43)

The field form is the Architect's choice, and the user approved it on 2026-11-10 (section 5). The D43 lab confirms
the field names before the build.

Six role groups and the shared group (the user, 2026-11-10):

| Group | Value | Members |
|---|---|---|
| Architect | `architect` | the Architect; the Architect executors, which write the MD files (specs, plans, briefs) and do other Architect tasks; the coherence validator of specs and plans; the cold reviewers; the main session |
| Engineering | `engineering` | the Executor, the cold reviewers, QA, Version Control, the DevOps advisor (D42); the supervisor in 0.5.0 |
| Lab | `lab` | `lab-researcher`, `lab-reader`, `lab-experimenter` |
| Security | `security` | the `security` agent; the Security Lead and the Security Executor in 0.5.0 |
| AI | `ai` | the AI lead (D45) |
| Council | `council` | the guild council (D47, a later release) |

- Agent definition: one frontmatter field `group`. Its value is one group, or a list of groups. Example:
  `group: engineering` in `agents/executor.md`.
- The cold reviewers are in two groups: Architect and Engineering (the user, 2026-11-10). The form is a list:
  `group: [engineering, architect]` in `agents/reviewer.md`. The Reviewer reviews code for Engineering. It reviews
  specs and plans for the Architect (spec review mode, D8). An agent with a list loads the skills of each group of
  its list.
- The main session is in the Architect group. The Architect runs there until 0.4.0 (D31). The main session has no
  agent definition. So the hook reads a skill call with no agent type as a call of the `architect` group. Not
  verified: how the hook input tells the main session from an agent. The D43 lab confirms it.
- Skill: one frontmatter field `groups`, a list of the same values. Example: `groups: [engineering]`.
- The shared group: the value `shared` in that list, alone. Example: `groups: [shared]` in the `ai-evals` skill.
  An agent of any group loads a `shared` skill.
- The hook passes a call of a `shared` skill. It also passes a call when one group of the agent is in the `groups`
  list of the skill.
- A skill of another plugin and a built-in skill have no `groups` field. The hook never refuses them.
- `@decided` (2026-11-10, the user; the Architect proposed it in the fifth cold review): a guilda skill always has a `groups` field. The approved text
  said that a skill with no `groups` field is not refused. It put the four user skills in that case. This item
  narrows that text to the skills that are not guilda skills. The four user skills and `guilda:lab` get
  `groups: [architect]`, because the main session is in the Architect group.
- How the hook finds the `groups` of a skill (`@decided` 2026-11-10). The `skill` value of the hook input has the form
  `<plugin>:<name>`. The prefix `guilda:` is a core skill: the hook reads `skills/<name>/SKILL.md` under the plugin
  root. The prefix of the extension, `guilda-skills:`, is an extension skill: the hook reads
  `plugins/guilda-skills/skills/<name>/SKILL.md` under the same root. Each other value is not a guilda skill, and the
  hook passes the call. The hook reads the frontmatter `groups` line of the file.
- Not verified: that the installed core folder holds `plugins/guilda-skills/`, and the exact `skill` value. The D43
  lab records both (section 2). When the folder is not there, `core/groups.json` holds the groups of each guilda
  skill.
- Fail closed for a guilda skill (`@decided` 2026-11-10). The hook refuses the call when the skill file does not exist or is
  not readable. It also refuses when the file has no `groups` field. It also refuses when the value is not a list
  of group values. The refusal text: "R30: cannot read the groups of the skill <skill name>. Add `groups` to its SKILL.md."
- Outside the group, the refusal text is: "R30: the skill <skill name> is not for the agent <agent type>. Ask
  the Architect for a skill of your group."
- Nobody is without a group (the user, 2026-11-10). The hook refuses a call of a guilda skill from an agent with
  no `group` field (fail closed). The refusal text: "R30: the agent <agent type> has no group field. Add `group` to
  its agent definition." `tests/roles.test.mjs` checks that each agent definition of guilda has the field (S37).
- The `group` of an agent: for the agent type `guilda:<name>`, the hook reads `agents/<name>.md` under the
  plugin root. Each other agent type has no `group` field for the hook.
- guilda guards only its own skills (the user, 2026-11-10, O10). A guilda skill is a skill of the core plugin or of
  the extension plugin. A built-in agent type, such as `general-purpose` or
  `Explore`, has no `group` field. An agent of another plugin has none too. Such an agent cannot load a guilda
  skill. The skills of other plugins and the built-in skills stay free for it.
- So each guilda role that calls a guilda skill needs an agent definition with a group. In 0.2.0 the core gets
  five more agent definitions, each with its `group` (the user, 2026-11-10). They are the Architect executor and
  the coherence validator (`architect`), and `lab-researcher`, `lab-reader` and `lab-experimenter` (`lab`). The
  first two are `architect-executor` and `coherence-validator` (the user, 2026-11-10, O3).
- Not verified: that `claude plugin validate --strict` accepts a frontmatter field that Claude Code does not know.
- The fallback: one file `core/groups.json`. Its form is
  `{ "main": ["architect"], "agents": { "guilda:executor": ["engineering"], "guilda:reviewer": ["engineering", "architect"] }, "skills": { "ai-evals": ["shared"] } }`.
  The hook reads this file, and the frontmatter gets no new field. A guilda skill or a `guilda:*` agent that the
  file does not list is refused, as above.

## 3f. Reuse of superpowers (D51)

The user decided on 2026-11-10: guilda reuses what `superpowers` 7.x (MIT) already has. The register has the full
list. Each path below is in the installed `superpowers` 7.0.0, seen on 2026-11-10.

Copied and adapted, because guilda needs its own format. Each copy keeps the MIT notice (S44). Each copy is in the
core plugin `guilda`, because `guilda:report`, `guilda:lab`, the Architect and the release step are in the core
(section 3c). No copy is in the extension or in `ios-on-linux`. The destination paths are the Architect's choice.

| Piece of `superpowers` | Path in `superpowers` | Copy in the core plugin `guilda` | Use in 0.2.0 |
|---|---|---|---|
| redaction policy | `skills/diagnosing-superpowers/references/redaction-policy.md` | `skills/report/references/redaction-policy.md` | `guilda:report` (D3) |
| scrub prompts | `skills/diagnosing-superpowers/prompts/scrub.md`, `skills/diagnosing-superpowers/prompts/scrub-audit.md` | `skills/report/prompts/scrub.md`, `skills/report/prompts/scrub-audit.md` | `guilda:report` (D3) |
| issue template | `skills/diagnosing-superpowers/templates/issue.md` | `skills/report/templates/issue.md`. The four `.github/ISSUE_TEMPLATE/report-<type>.md` files are adapted from it too | `guilda:report` (D3) |
| `case.md` | `skills/diagnosing-superpowers/templates/case.md` | `skills/report/templates/case.md` | the field report (D3) |
| `report.md` | `skills/diagnosing-superpowers/templates/report.md` | `skills/lab/templates/report.md` | the lab report (D34) |
| `task-brief`, `review-package`, `sdd-workspace` | `skills/subagent-driven-development/scripts/` | `skills/architect/scripts/task-brief`, `skills/architect/scripts/review-package`, `skills/architect/scripts/sdd-workspace` | the `Brief file:` line, and the diff file for Security (D5). The other four scripts call `sdd-workspace` for the output folder |
| `task-start`, `task-done` | `skills/executing-plans/scripts/` | `skills/architect/scripts/task-start`, `skills/architect/scripts/task-done` | the `Base commit:` field (U6) |
| `bump-version.sh` | `scripts/bump-version.sh` | `scripts/bump-version.mjs`, a rewrite in Node | the release step (O9, section 3g) |

`THIRD-PARTY-NOTICES.md` lists each path of the third column: 16 files, with the four issue templates of
`.github/ISSUE_TEMPLATE/`.

The changes in the five script copies (the Architect's choice, fifth cold review). The originals are `bash`
scripts, and the copies stay `bash` scripts:

- Output folder. `sdd-workspace` of `superpowers` prints `<repository root>/.superpowers/sdd/<plan name>/`. The copy
  prints `<repository root>/.guilda/work/<plan name>/`. The plan name is the file name of the plan without `.md` and
  without `.plan`. So `login.md` and `login.plan.md` share one folder.
- Git-ignored. The copy keeps the method of the original. It writes `.guilda/work/.gitignore` with the one line
  `*`. It writes that file only when the file does not exist. So the folder ignores itself, and the script changes no committed file.
- No project layer. With no `.guilda/` folder, the copy prints `guilda is not set up`, exits 2 and creates no
  folder (R28).
- Plan file. The original takes a plan file of `docs/superpowers/plans/`. The copy takes the feature MD or its
  `<feature>.plan.md` (R4).
- Headings. The original finds a task by a heading `Task <N>`. A guilda plan has `## Plan: Part N` sections, and
  each task heading is `### T<n> — <title>`, as in the 0.1.1 plan. The copy of `task-brief` takes the task ID
  `T<n>`. It prints the lines from that heading to the next heading of the same level or a higher level. It reads
  no heading inside a code fence.
- Parts. One file can have the same task ID in two `## Plan: Part N` sections. Then `task-brief` exits 3 and names
  the parts. The caller gives the part: `task-brief <plan file> <task ID> --part <N>`.
- Script paths. `task-start` and `task-done` of the original call
  `../../subagent-driven-development/scripts/task-brief` and `sdd-workspace`. In the copy, the five scripts are in
  one folder, `skills/architect/scripts/`. Each one calls the others in its own folder, through `bash`.
- File names in the output folder: `<task ID>-brief.md`, `review-<base>..<head>.diff`, `<task ID>-tests.log` and
  `progress.md`. They are the names of the original, with the task ID of guilda.

Called from `superpowers`, with no copy: the skills and the files that the register lists under D51. In 0.2.0 they
serve EXE2, R27, R6, R11, D8, D9, D10, D28, D41 and D43.

- The core plugin has no dependency (D40). So a core file names a `superpowers` skill only as an option: "use it
  when it is installed". `skills/init/SKILL.md` does this today for `superpowers:brainstorming`. The core text stays
  complete without it. This is the Architect's reading, and open question O8 asks the user.
- guilda never calls `superpowers:writing-plans`. It writes to `docs/superpowers/plans/`, and R4 refuses that
  folder.
- The `superpowers` skills have no `groups` field, so the D43 hook does not refuse them (section 3e).

## 3g. One version for the three plugins (D51, O9)

The user decided on 2026-11-10.

- The three plugins share one version: the core `guilda`, the extension and `ios-on-linux`. They bump together in
  each release, also when only one of them changed.
- The bump script updates each of them. It is `scripts/bump-version.mjs`. It is a rewrite in Node of
  `scripts/bump-version.sh` of `superpowers` (the user, 2026-11-10). It keeps the MIT notice and the credit to
  `superpowers` (section 3f, S44).
- The script reads `.version-bump.json` at the repository root. That file is guilda's own. It lists the `version`
  field of the three plugin manifests: `.claude-plugin/plugin.json`,
  `plugins/guilda-skills/.claude-plugin/plugin.json` and `plugins/ios-on-linux/.claude-plugin/plugin.json`.
- `.claude-plugin/marketplace.json` has no `version` field today. If a plugin entry gets one, `.version-bump.json`
  lists it too.
- The commands, as in the script of `superpowers` 7.0.0: `node scripts/bump-version.mjs <version>` writes the version
  in each listed file. `--check` prints the version of each file, and it exits 1 when they differ. `--audit` also
  searches the repository for the current version string.
- The differences from the original (the Architect's choice, fifth cold review):
  - The version must match `^\d+\.\d+\.\d+$`. The original accepts each text that starts with that form. Another
    value is an error: exit 1, and no file changes.
  - A listed file that does not exist is an error in each command: exit 1, with the path. The original prints
    `SKIP` for it in a bump. The bump reads each listed file before it writes one, so an error changes no file.
  - A bump does not run the audit after the write. The release step runs `--check` (`RELEASING.md`).
  - `--audit` runs the check, then the search. It prints each file that has the current version string and is
    not a listed file. It exits 1 when the check fails or when the search prints a file. In the other cases it
    exits 0. The original always exits 0 here.
  - The exclude list is the `audit.exclude` list of `.version-bump.json`: `CHANGELOG.md`, `ROADMAP.md`,
    `RELEASING.md`, `README.md`, `scripts/changelog.mjs`, `.guilda`, `docs`, `tests`, `evals`,
    `node_modules`, `.git` and `.version-bump.json`. `docs` and `tests` are there because the specs and the
    fixtures name old versions. The five files after `CHANGELOG.md` name a version in their text (checked with
    `grep -rlF` on 2026-11-10).
- The script needs only Node, with no `jq` and no `bash`. So `npm test` tests it. Only the release step runs it. No
  hook and no skill runs it.
- `RELEASING.md` changes with it. Step 1 names the shared version and the script. Step 5 creates one tag for each
  plugin, `<plugin name>--v<version>` (D40). Not verified: that `claude plugin tag` works for a plugin in a
  subfolder. The build checks it.
- The version ranges of the extension dependencies are not guilda versions. The script does not change them.

## 3h. The always-on context budget

`@decided` (2026-11-10, the user). The short form of the `gate` and `judgment` rules is the Architect's choice,
from the measure of the later releases (below).

- The always-on context is the whole text that the session start puts in each session: the `guilda` lines, the
  core rules, `core/writing.md` and the project rules. Its limit stays: 150 non-blank lines, with a project rules
  file of 20 lines. It is a design invariant. The test is 'context has 150 non-blank lines or fewer with a
  20-line project rules file' of `tests/session-start.test.mjs`. No task raises this limit, and no task lowers
  the allowance of 20 lines for the project rules. The rules template of `guilda:init` stays at 20 non-blank
  lines or fewer, so a new project starts inside the allowance.
- The context has the short form of each core rule. The full block of each rule stays in `core/rules.md`, with
  its heading and its `Rule:`, `Why:` and `Check:` lines.
- Short form of a rule with the level `law`: one line. It is the heading of the rule, then
  ` (law — enforced by a hook)`. Example:
  `### R19 — No secrets or absolute paths in commits (law — enforced by a hook)`. A hook enforces the rule, and
  the refusal of the hook names the rule ID.
- Short form of a rule with the level `gate` or `judgment`: two lines. The first is the heading, then the level
  in parentheses. The second is the `Rule:` line, word for word. Example: `### R1 — Source of truth (gate)`, then
  the `Rule:` line of R1. No rule that an agent must follow from text loses its `Rule:` line.
- The `Why:` and `Check:` lines are not in the context. One line after the rules tells the agent where they are:
  `Full rule blocks: <plugin root>/core/rules.md`. An agent reads the full block on demand: before it cites the
  rule in a verdict, and after a refusal of a hook.
- The intro lines of `core/rules.md` stay in the context as they are (3 lines today).
- The change: `scripts/lib/rules-context.mjs` (new) has `contextRules(text)`. It returns the text of
  `core/rules.md` in the short form. `scripts/hooks/session-start.mjs` puts `contextRules(...)` and the
  `Full rule blocks:` line in the context, in the valid and in the broken project layer. `core/subagent.md` does
  not change, and the subagent start still prints it in full: a subagent gets its four full blocks. (The
  subagent start gets its `Process:` part in section 3d.)
- Two limits, two texts. The limit of `tests/core-content.test.mjs` counts the two full files, `core/rules.md` +
  `core/writing.md`: 142 lines (section 3b). The limit of `tests/session-start.test.mjs` counts the context, which
  has the short form: 150 lines.

The count of the context, in non-blank lines. The fixture has a project rules file of 20 lines and a version notice
of one line:

| Stage | Full files (`rules.md` + `writing.md`) | Rules: `law` / other | Core rules in the context | Context |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 0.1.1 (today, full blocks) | 103 + 19 = 122 | 3 / 22 | 103 | 147 |
| after R26 and R27, with the short form | 111 + 19 = 130 | 3 / 24 | 3 + 3 + 48 + 1 = 55 | 99 |
| after the `Process:` line (section 3d) | 130 | 3 / 24 | 55 | 100 |
| after R28 | 134 | 4 / 24 | 56 | 101 |
| after R29 | 138 | 5 / 24 | 57 | 102 |
| after R30 | 142 | 6 / 24 | 58 | 103 |

The context of 0.2.0 has 103 lines with a version notice and 102 without one. Its parts: the `guilda <version>
active` line (1), the core rules (58: 3 intro lines, 6 `law` lines, 24 rules of 2 lines, and the `Full rule
blocks:` line), `core/writing.md` (19), the project rules (20), the `Languages:` and `Tiers:` line (1), the
`Process:` line (1), the diary command (1), the glossary path (1) and the version notice (1, only after an
update). With full blocks the context would have 168 lines. One more line can show: the notice of the extension
(S13), only when the extension did not load. The context then has 104 lines.

Room for the later releases: 47 lines under the limit. A later `law` rule costs one line, and each other rule
costs two. The owner of the 0.3.0 to 0.5.0 specs measured about 25 more lines with full blocks for the rules of
those releases; in the short form they cost less. S47 pins 110 lines for 0.2.0, so that a later release sees its
own growth in a test.

## 4. Classification of commands and skills (D24)

| Name | Kind | Who runs it |
|---|---|---|
| `guilda:architect`, `guilda:init`, `guilda:update`, `guilda:report` | skill | the user, in a conversation |
| `guilda:lab` | skill | the Architect, to write a lab brief and dispatch the lab agent (D19). Not a user command. |
| `doctor` (`scripts/doctor.mjs`) | internal script | the activation check (D2): the Architect before the first dispatch of a session, and `guilda:init` at its end (section 4a); from 0.3.0 the mod also shows it |
| `report-send` (`scripts/report-send.mjs`) | internal script | `guilda:report` (D3): the R19 scan of the final text, then the send with `gh`, or the save in `.guilda/reports/` (section 4a) |
| `bump-version` (`scripts/bump-version.mjs`) | internal script | the release step of `RELEASING.md` (O9, section 3g). No hook and no skill runs it. |
| `status` | internal script | 0.3.0 (D21). Not in 0.2.0. |
| `send` | internal script | 0.4.0 (D17). Not in 0.2.0. |
| `watch`, `tick` | internal scripts | 0.5.0 (D15). Not in 0.2.0. |
| pace | internal script and the plan step of the Architect | 0.5.0 (D22). No `guilda:pace` skill. |
| `guilda:consolidate <name>` | code marker (D9) | the Executor writes it, a later task finds it with grep |

## 4a. Contracts of the internal scripts (the Architect's choice)

### `doctor`

Command: `node scripts/doctor.mjs <projectDir> [--session <id>] [--agents <name,name,...>] [--new-project]`. It
prints one line for each check: `<check>: PASS|FAIL|UNKNOWN — <evidence>`. It exits 1 when a line is FAIL, and 0 in
the other cases. So a run with UNKNOWN lines and no FAIL line exits 0.

| Check | Evidence source | PASS | FAIL | UNKNOWN |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| core plugin loaded | `.claude-plugin/plugin.json` of the plugin root that holds the script | the name is `guilda`, and the line shows the version | the manifest is not readable | never |
| hooks live | the marker `.guilda/.hooks-live.json` | its `sessionId` is equal to `--session` | no marker in a project that has a `.guilda/` folder, in a run with no `--new-project` | no `--session`, another session ID, or no `.guilda/` folder; the line shows the marker time. Also no marker in a run with `--new-project`; the evidence is "no marker yet; the session start writes it in the next session" |
| `guilda:*` agents present | the `--agents` list, against the file names of the core `agents/` folder | the list has each agent of the folder | the list misses one; the line names it | no `--agents` |
| extension plugin loaded | the method of the O1 lab | as the lab decides | as the lab decides | always, on a NO-GO or MORE DATA of the lab; the evidence is "no method" |
| project files present | `projectState`, and `.guilda/rules.md` on disk | a valid project layer and the rules file | no `.guilda/` folder, or a broken project layer; the line shows the reason | never |
| version | the installed version, `guildaVersion` and `schemaVersion` of the config | the config is not older | never | the config is older; the line says "Run guilda:update". Also no config, or a broken project layer; the evidence is "no config to compare" |

- The marker: `scripts/hooks/session-start.mjs` writes `.guilda/.hooks-live.json` at each run in a project that
  has a `.guilda/` folder. The content is `{ "sessionId": "<session_id of the hook input>", "version": "<installed>", "at": "<ISO time>" }`.
  With no `.guilda/` folder the hook writes nothing (section 3d).
- `--new-project` (the Architect's choice, fifth cold review): `guilda:init` gives it when it created the
  `.guilda/` folder in this session. The session start ran before the folder existed, so it wrote no marker. That
  is not a failure. Without the option, a missing marker is FAIL: the hooks did not run in a project that was set
  up.
- The version line (the Architect's choice, fifth cold review). The config is older when its `guildaVersion` is
  lower than the installed version. `compareSemver` of `scripts/lib/version.mjs` compares them. It is also older when its
  `schemaVersion` is lower than the constant of `scripts/lib/project.mjs`. A missing `schemaVersion` reads as 1.
  The line compares `schemaVersion` too. A config that someone edited by hand can have the new `guildaVersion`
  and the old schema. `--migrate` corrects both. With no config or with a broken project layer, the line is
  UNKNOWN. Then the "project files present" line has the FAIL.
- The ignore line: `IGNORED` in `scripts/lib/violations.mjs` gets the marker name. The session start calls
  `ensureIgnored` before it writes the marker. Today it does not call it. So a 0.1.x project gets the line in
  `.guilda/.gitignore` at its first 0.2.0 session. It does not commit the marker. If the line cannot be
  written, the hook writes no marker.
- `.guilda/.gitignore` is the one committed file that a hook can change (the Architect's choice, fifth cold
  review). The change is only the append of a missing ignore line by `ensureIgnored`, and a second run adds
  nothing. A hook of 0.1.x already does it at the first violation record. Each other committed file stays as the
  migration contract says (section 3d, S18). The other option was a marker outside the project folder. It was not
  chosen, because `doctor` and each project would need a second place for hook state.
- The `--agents` form: each name is as the `Agent` tool lists it, for example `guilda:executor`. `doctor` removes
  the prefix `guilda:`. It compares the rest with each file name of `agents/` without `.md`. It ignores a name with
  no `guilda:` prefix.
- The order: the O1 lab runs before the `doctor` task (section 2, Labs). On a GO, the task builds the extension
  line with the method of the lab. On a NO-GO or MORE DATA, the line is always UNKNOWN.
- The caller: a script cannot see the session. So the caller gives `--session` and `--agents`. The Architect reads
  the agent types of its `Agent` tool for `--agents`. Not verified: that a skill can read the session ID
  (`${CLAUDE_SESSION_ID}`). Without it the hooks line is UNKNOWN with the marker time, and the caller compares the time.
- When: the Architect runs it before the first dispatch of a session (D2). `guilda:init` runs it at its end, with
  `--new-project` when it created the `.guilda/` folder. Each one shows the lines that are not PASS to the user.
- The test fixture for "hooks live" is a temporary project with a valid config. Its marker file has the
  `sessionId` `s1`. The test runs `doctor` with `--session s1`, then with `--session s2`, then with the marker removed.
  The last run has the marker removed and `--new-project`.

### `report-send`

Command: `node scripts/report-send.mjs <projectDir> <reportFile> --type <improvement|bug|feature|other> [--repo <owner/name>]`.

- Input: `<reportFile>` is a Markdown file. Its first line is `# <title>`. The title is the text after `# `. The
  body is the rest of the file, without the title line and without the blank lines directly under it. A file with
  another first line is a usage error (exit 1).
- Type: `--type` is required. A value outside the four is a usage error (exit 1).
- Project layer: with no `.guilda/` folder, guilda is not set up (R28). The script prints
  `refused: guilda is not set up`, then the R28 notice text of section 3a, and exits 1. It saves nothing and creates
  no folder. In a broken project layer the script uses the default repository.
- Target repository: `--repo`, then `report.repo` of `.guilda/config.json`, then the default. The default is the
  `repository` value of the core `.claude-plugin/plugin.json`, which is this repository.
- Repository form: a value is `owner/name`, or `https://github.com/owner/name` with or without a last `/` or
  `.git`. The script changes the URL form to `owner/name`, because `gh --repo` takes that form. The default is a
  URL, so it passes through this change. Another value is invalid. An invalid `--repo` is a usage error (exit 1).
  With an invalid `report.repo`, the script saves the report and sends nothing (section 3d).
- Labels: `report-improvement`, `report-bug`, `report-feature`, `report-other`. Each label exists in this
  repository, with one issue template `.github/ISSUE_TEMPLATE/report-<type>.md`.
- Send: `gh issue create --repo <owner/name> --title <title> --body-file - --label <label>`. The script gives the
  body on standard input. So the issue body does not repeat the title line.
- Another repository (the Architect's choice, fifth cold review): `gh issue create` fails when the label does not
  exist in the target. The script cannot know the labels of another repository. So the command has `--label` only
  when the target is equal to the default repository, after the change to `owner/name`. For each other target the
  command has no `--label`. Then the second output line is
  `note: sent without a label, because <owner/name> is not the default repository`. `guilda:report` shows that
  line to the user.
- The scan: the script scans the full report file, with the title line, before a send or a save. `scanText` of
  `scripts/lib/scan.mjs` gives each hit as a kind (`secret` or `abs-path`) and a line number. Line 1 is the title
  line.
- Output, first line: `sent: <issue URL>` (exit 0), `saved: <path>` (exit 0), `refused: R19 <hit>` (exit 1), or
  `refused: guilda is not set up` (exit 1). The form of `<hit>` is `<kind> in line <n>`, for the first hit. An
  example is `refused: R19 secret in line 1`. The line never has the text of the hit.
- A save goes to `.guilda/reports/<date>-<type>-<slug of the title>.md`. The saved file is the full report file,
  with its title line. The script saves when `gh` is missing, when `gh auth status` fails, and when
  `gh issue create` fails.
- The saved name (the Architect's choice, fifth cold review). `<date>` is the UTC date in the form `YYYY-MM-DD`.
  The slug is the title in lower case. Each group of characters outside `a-z` and `0-9` becomes one `-`. The slug
  has no `-` at its start or its end, and it has 40 characters or fewer. An empty slug becomes `report`. When the
  name exists, the script adds `-2` before `.md`, then `-3`, until the name is free. It never writes over a saved
  report.
- `.guilda/reports/` is git-ignored (the user, 2026-11-10). `IGNORED` gets the line `reports/`, and the script calls `ensureIgnored` before
  a save. A saved report is text for another repository, not project content.

## 5. Approval checklist

Each `@proposed` or `@open` item in the 0.2.0 scope, as one question with a yes/no answer or two options. The
Architect asks them with the question tool (D23), one decision at a time, and puts the recommended answer first.

### User decisions with two options

Each item gives the question, then the answer of the user.

- [x] U1 (D1). Question: the register said that the levels enter a release only after E1 runs. But 0.2.0 ships
  `light` and `full` before E1. Answer (2026-11-10): 0.2.0 ships the two levels, and E1 sets their gates later.
- [x] U2 (D2, D28, S13). Question: without the official marketplace, guilda did not load on the first install, and no
  warning showed. Answer (2026-11-10): `skill-creator` (anthropics/skills) and `superpowers` (obra/superpowers) are
  required dependencies of the extension plugin only. The core plugin always loads, with no dependency and the laws
  on. It tells the user when the extension did not load (D40). The README gives one install command that adds
  both marketplaces, then guilda, and says how to check that guilda loaded. `guilda:init` checks both dependencies.
- [x] U3 (CHANGELOG). Question: a 0.1.0 install that updates straight to 0.2.0 does not see the notes of 0.1.1.
  Answer (2026-11-10, with D40): the 0.2.0 session start detects a project layer of 0.1.x and migrates it. It asks
  the user only when a value cannot be inferred. No project runs `guilda:init` again. (2026-11-10, section 3d) The
  hook asks nothing. A value that cannot be inferred becomes a notice, and `guilda:update` asks.

  Answer (2026-11-10): the role is renamed `supervisor`; "lead" is not a forbidden word; the Security Lead keeps its
  name.

  and the Security Lead.

  brief. The ARC6 hook refuses a new brief without it. The 0.2.0 CHANGELOG lists this as a change of the brief
  format. The tolerant reader of D40 covers stored files only, not a brief written at dispatch.
